## Objective and operating agreement

On 2026-10-22 the user requested autonomous, repeated development toward usefulness
and quality comparable to Adobe Acrobat or Foxit. Continue implementation,
verification and review without routine permission checkpoints. Preserve local
documents and existing work. No parity claim is justified by a feature count.

Maintain this record between cycles. Each cycle selects a user-visible gap,
defines an acceptance workflow, implements it, tests native output where relevant,
reviews material risks, and updates this record. Leave the overarching goal active
while material gaps remain. Build runnable checkpoints; do not restart a user's
open editor or overwrite source PDFs to test them.

Source glyph selection, text removal and drawing removal work with these admitted images; existing text-only image admission remains unchanged.

### Previous tagged-page duplication checkpoint

Duplicating supported tagged PDF pages now saves independent page, annotation and tag ownership. Self-links follow the copy; other local links and source bookmarks keep the first occurrence. Each copied table's Header references resolve to its own header IDs, including indirect attributes, streams, arrays and ClassMap aliases. Supported page-local comment replies no longer depend on annotation order. Original authored logical order remains intact, with copied subtrees appended in duplication order.

Corrected verification: **228 passing native executions / 225 distinct passed tests**, with eight remaining unexecuted ignored cases. TypeScript/Vite and GUI/CLI debug builds passed. Windows acceptance covered Duplicate/Undo/Redo/Save/reopen, bookmark navigation, independent copy rotation and combined-source duplication. Independent pypdf/PyMuPDF audits checked **12 saved outputs** (four authored native, five table-attribute variants and three desktop outputs), including exact 1x/2x pixels and raw text/images. Six deliberate corruptions were rejected, with two unchanged-rewrite controls accepted. The single final review's two findings were reproduced and corrected locally. A pypdf alias-cache failure in the new audit was diagnosed and corrected in the audit only; original failed evidence and unchanged PDF bytes remain recorded.

Local candidate: `artifacts/duplicate-tagged-pages-20261030/build-2/candidate`. Both pre-review and corrected evidence are preserved; the existing portable package is unchanged.

Next: combined forms and added/imported overlays in graph-preserving Save. Empty tags with explicit or inherited page ownership are supported. Native-only bookmark editing, canvas hyperlink activation, broader producer/resource acceptance, all-target regression, printing and packaging remain separate work. This checkpoint is not accessibility, release or Adobe/Foxit parity certification; the broad goal remains active.

### Previous deleted-page navigation checkpoint

Deleting supported unique PDF pages now preserves surviving native bookmarks and links. Native bookmark objects retain raw titles/styles/private metadata, and targeted parents with surviving children become folders. Name and String destination namespaces stay separate. The current selected bookmark projection is recognized without authorizing unrelated native-only edits.

Verification: 208 passing native executions represent 206 distinct passed tests; seven distinct tests remain ignored. Fresh TypeScript/Vite and GUI/CLI debug builds passed. Windows acceptance covered deletion, Undo/Redo, Save/reopen and keyboard bookmark navigation. Six deliberate corruptions were rejected. The one final scoped review found no concrete defects.

### Previous selected/reordered-page checkpoint

Graph-preserving Save now supports unique selected, reordered and interleaved pages from admitted tagged sources. It retains page contents and resources, materializes inherited geometry, preserves authored logical order, and rebuilds exact surviving tag ownership. Current whole-source bookmarks follow selections and reorders without reverting to historical titles. Any surviving reference to a removed page/tag is refused; repeated occurrences, unsupported partial structure attributes and combined forms/overlays still need separate adapters.

The affected native gates, three authored Word/Pictures outputs, fresh frontend and GUI/CLI debug builds, and Windows organizer workflows passed. Desktop acceptance covered page deletion, Undo/Redo, combined reordering, Save/reopen and keyboard bookmark targets at the new page positions. ### Previous runnable embedded-font checkpoint

Existing-text removal now supports verified embedded simple TrueType and
horizontal Identity-H/CIDFontType2 Latin fonts. Accented text and multibyte source
codes keep their original typography and following positions. Shared fonts and
program streams are reused across aliases/pages and distinct resource mappings.

The [embedded-font checkpoint](../artifacts/embedded-text-removal-20261030/checkpoint.json)
records99focused native checks,684library passes/four existing skips, a corrected
debug build and the unchanged1367-test frontend gate. Actual desktop acceptance
covers both font formats, selected duplicates, Cancel, Undo/Redo, save/reopen and
accented-text re-edit. Independent readers passed48desktop comparisons/four bad
controls, plus8native-corpus comparisons/16controls including spacing and scaling.
Both resource-sharing review findings were reproduced and corrected.

Next: broader producer-document acceptance, remaining graphics/annotation
compatibility and clearer refusal guidance, then optimized portable integration.
Unsupported page/content/font types remain explicit limits; this is text removal,
not certified secure area redaction. No release or commercial-parity claim.

### Earlier visible text-removal checkpoint

The desktop now exposes a scoped **Remove text…** workflow: mark existing text,
compare the current edited page with the candidate, acknowledge disclosed
omissions, Apply once, Undo/Redo and save a copy. It captures the exact tab/history
and retains original sources. Cancel, expired working-copy refresh, malformed
bookmark data and failed glyph verification are guarded.

The [workflow checkpoint](../artifacts/text-removal-workflow-20261030/checkpoint.json)
records 28 focused tests, 1367 frontend passes across 205 files, a fresh debug
desktop build, and actual Preview/Cancel/Apply/Undo/Redo/save/reopen acceptance.
Two independent readers passed 26 comparisons and rejected two wrong-duplicate
controls. Both review findings were reproduced and corrected. The owned test app
was stopped; the user's app and source PDFs were preserved.

This remains text-only removal for a strict supported subset. Embedded fonts,
annotations/forms and other unsupported contents may be refused. Next: improve
common-document compatibility and refusal guidance while retaining fidelity
proofs, then optimized portable integration and broader acceptance. No secure
area-redaction, arbitrary-PDF, release or commercial-parity claim.

### Earlier edited-document composition checkpoint

Text-removal preparation now composes the current edited document through Save:
ordered/duplicate pages, overlays, crops/rotation, forms and bookmark state. Native
sessions own this immutable working copy and recheck original inputs before preview
or Apply. Captured font/image resources and allocation-based cleanup are covered.
Potential metadata/navigation loss from page-import composition is recorded before
it happens and carried to the preview for disclosure. Unsupported content is kept
in the working copy; strict removal may still refuse it.

The [composition checkpoint](../artifacts/redaction-composition-20261030/checkpoint.json)
records85focused checks,670library passes/four existing skips,1339frontend tests,
native binary checking and TypeScript/Vite build. Independent readers verified
ordering,duplicate-only removal,retained form answer/bookmark and unchanged pixels
outside source-defined masks; three bad-output controls were rejected. Final review
found no actionable defects. Native bounds are retained-byte limits, not peak RSS.

Next: exact tab/history binding, visible marking and preview, one-step Apply/Undo,
explicit omission disclosure and atomic save-copy. Keep every original source alive
through history. Transformed/custom-resource independent fixtures and real desktop
window/IPC acceptance remain open. No new executable/package or parity claim.

### Earlier trusted desktop host checkpoint

The native text-removal engine is now connected to six desktop commands through
trusted window-owned sessions and a typed optional adapter. A closing window
invalidates queued work immediately; pending previews are released, while applied
results survive dialog release for history and are reclaimed on document/window
closure. Native owner credentials stay in the host. No visible tool is exposed
until current-document composition and UI/save-copy acceptance are complete.

The [host checkpoint](../artifacts/redaction-host-20261030/checkpoint.json) records
72focused native checks (9host),657library passes/four existing skips,
1338frontend passes,TypeScript/Vite build and native binary checking. Independent
review found no demonstrated defect. Initial UI/native timing failures remain in
the evidence; unchanged assertions passed after reducing test concurrency.
Real IPC loss/window lifecycle and exhaustive interleavings remain unverified.

Next: compose the actual edited snapshot, preserve page occurrence identity and
overlays, then visible marking, preview, Apply/history and atomic save-copy.
Unsupported content must be refused explicitly, never silently omitted. This is
text-only removal, not certified secure area redaction. No executable/package
changed, and no commercial parity or whole-process memory claim is made.

### Earlier aggregate loader checkpoint

Text-removal inspection now loads through an opt-in aggregate resource budget:
64MiB input/decoded data,128MiB copied object payload,1million parser attempts,
200,000 xref/index tokens and64indirect-resolution levels. Charges occur before
allocation/growth and remain sticky across ignored errors. The bounded path
handles compressed object streams, incremental sections and checked predictors;
ordinary reading retains its recovery behavior. Failed decoder recovery and the
nonstandard Brotli large-window extension are refused for structural rewriting.
The [loader checkpoint](../artifacts/redaction-loader-budget-20261029/checkpoint.json)
binds648library passes (four existing ignored),63focused Rayon passes and128vendor
passes (one upstream fixture absent). Review's swallowed-limit defect was
reproduced and fixed. All240source mappings/11,424glyphs,48saved outputs and the
three-page preview remain equal to earlier independently audited results.
Vendored parser sources are now included in ordinary build fingerprints.

These are logical allocation/work limits, not an exact process-memory or native
PDFium safety guarantee. Next is the trusted host session/ownership adapter and
composition/overlay contract, followed by visible marking, preview, Apply and
save-copy acceptance. Public redaction and commercial parity remain unclaimed;
no executable or portable package changed.

### Earlier native preview checkpoint

The private native engine now supports multi-page text-removal previews with
session ownership, paginated measured bounds, one-shot Apply, Discard, expiry,
and cleanup on source/session closure or abandoned replies. Overlapping marks
select each glyph once; all pages are removed in one source-bound transaction.
Pending candidate allocations are bounded and cannot become sources of other
pending previews. Adopted results survive original-source and session closure.
The [preview checkpoint](../artifacts/redaction-preview-20261029/checkpoint.json)
binds 49 focused passes, 634 library passes (four existing ignored), a three-page
independent PDFium/MuPDF/pypdf audit with zero outside pixels, three rejected
negative checks, and unchanged replay of 240 mappings/11,424 glyphs and48saved
outputs. Final scoped review found no actionable correctness defects.

This is native integration, not an exposed redaction feature. Next: aggregate
initial-parser memory hardening, trusted host session ownership, composition and
overlay rules, then visible marking/preview/Apply/save-copy acceptance. Bounds
select whole text glyphs, not every kind of content inside an area. Protected
publication and broader fonts/content remain open. No executable or portable
package was rebuilt. Existing accepted editing capabilities and packages remain.

### Earlier source-selection checkpoint

The private engine now creates source-owned text marks from actual PDFium glyph
bounds and applies them only after source identity and current edit/copy rights
are rechecked. Unique Unicode/origin mapping preserves original byte offsets;
ambiguous glyphs, stale/closed sources, invalid marks and protected policies refuse.
Interior spaces can join selected letters; unrelated source text is not bridged.
The [selection checkpoint](../artifacts/redaction-selection-20261029/checkpoint.json)
binds 38 focused passes, 623 library passes (four existing ignored), 240 mapped
sources with 11,424 glyphs, four rejected mapping mutations and 48 independently
verified mark saves. All earlier 482 fixture files remain byte-identical, and the
48 mark outputs equal prior accepted range outputs. Final review found no
correctness defects.

This remains a private runtime transaction. Next: native multipage/multiblock
coverage alongside explicit page projection, bounded preview ownership and visible
marking. Tight boxes select whole text glyphs; they are not all-content area or
pixel masks. Aggregate hostile-parser limits, protected publication and broader
fonts/content remain gaps before general public exposure. No executable or package
was rebuilt in this milestone.

## Direction confirmed after PDFCraft comparison, 2026-10-26

The user tried PDFCraft and reported that existing-PDF text editing did not work in
that workflow, while Folio's did. Continue Folio as the product. This is a concrete
user report, not proof that PDFCraft cannot edit any existing PDF. Keep faithful
existing-text and paragraph editing central; selectively learn from PDFCraft's
font handling, regression cases and automation rather than change foundations.

The user particularly values CLI/MCP automation. The first CLI milestone is now
implemented and verified: `folio-cli info`, `text`, and `render` use the shared
native engine and the same desktop text/image exporters. See docs/automation.md.
The local stdio MCP implementation now exposes inspection/text export/page rendering
through the same CLI executable. Explicit launch inputs become immutable snapshots;
tools address IDs and use generated paths under the configured export parent.
Each operation runs a short-lived native CLI worker. Saved-file automation does
not attach to unsaved desktop tabs or provide a persistent editing session.
Existing-text inspection, validated replacement preview and exact new-copy saving
are now implemented. Native preservation proofs remain mandatory.

### Saved original-font text re-editing, 2026-10-28

The [saved-text plan](superpowers/plans/2026-10-28-dependent-text-reedit.md) is
complete as a local milestone. Supported text can grow or shrink after saving
and reopening while retaining its embedded font, original spacing and the
position of following text. Repeated edits adjust the existing cursor correction
instead of accumulating instructions. No external font import is required when
the saved font already covers the replacement. Fit, glyph, clipping, permissions
and surrounding-content checks remain mandatory.

## Reference capabilities

Reference documentation inspected on 2026-10-22:

- [Adobe text editing](https://helpx.adobe.com/acrobat/using/edit-text-pdfs1.html):
  edit existing text with paragraph reflow inside independent text boxes.
- [Adobe text box adjustment](https://helpx.adobe.com/acrobat/desktop/edit-documents/edit-text-in-pdfs/adjust-text-boxes.html):
  move/resize/rotate boxes; resizing reflows at the same font size.
- [Foxit PDF Editor](https://www.foxit.com/PDF-editor/): editing, page organization,
  OCR, forms, signing and document protection form a broad workflow benchmark.

These are capability references, not measured performance comparisons or claims
that Folio matches either application. Cloud collaboration and commercial account
services are not prerequisites for this local application's everyday usefulness.

### Acceptance checklist for the overall objective

This checklist describes outstanding evidence, not a claim that an unlisted
workflow is unnecessary or that a feature count establishes Adobe/Foxit parity.
The dated checkpoints above prove bounded cases; each broader requirement needs
evidence at its own scope. Update this checklist from measured workflows rather
than marking it complete when implementation alone exists.

- [ ] **Existing-document editing:** run representative unchanged producer files
  through single text, grouped text, mixed paragraphs, nested objects and image
  edits; add and delete content, save, reopen and edit again. Verify text, fonts,
  glyph positions, untouched pixels and original source integrity independently.
  Include the actual quiz and documents beyond the small current producer corpus.
  Record unsupported cases and test that refusals keep the user's draft intact.
- [ ] **Document fidelity:** combine edits with existing forms, notes, links,
  bookmarks, metadata, tags, encryption, page crops and rotations. Independently
  verify the saved relationships and later behavior, not just a matching raster.
  Keep source-resource removal and legacy extraction-order limitations explicit.
- [ ] **Everyday end-to-end use:** exercise reading/search, organization, form
  filling/authoring, OCR, review, image editing and exports together across dirty
  tabs. Test Cancel, Undo/Redo, reopen and crash recovery, including interrupted
  saves and destination conflicts, with no cross-document ownership or data loss.
- [ ] **Remaining major capabilities:** implement and independently validate true
  redaction and certificate-based signing/verification before claiming them.
  Drawn signature images and opaque drawing remain separate existing features.
  Broader scripts, font programs, form calculations/XFA and complex conversion
  remain open scope decisions and compatibility gaps, not completed capabilities.
- [ ] **Desktop usability and accessibility:** complete keyboard-only workflows,
  focus restoration, screen-reader checks, small windows and display scaling on
  the integrated build. Inspect completed states and error recovery, not only
  successful API responses or screenshots captured while loading.
- [ ] **Performance and resource behavior:** measure large/dense/scanned PDFs,
  many pages/fields, repeated edits and long sessions. Record latency, retained
  memory and cancellation behavior. Verify actual low-memory hardware and physical
  printing separately; tests on this development machine do not prove either.
- [ ] **Automation and delivery:** prove corresponding desktop/CLI/MCP edits have
  the intended shared behavior; verify authorization, stale selections, interrupted
  clients and artifact cleanup. Validate a fresh portable package outside the
  checkout with independent readers and retained originals. Keep exact source,
  binary and evidence identities, and leave known failures visible.

## Cycle order

1. Paragraph editing usability: original page with selectable text, visible area,
   zoom and original/edited comparison. Preserve preview ownership and one-step undo.
2. Document organization: extract selected pages/ranges and create/insert pages.
3. Paragraph compatibility and annotation coexistence, driven by native fixtures.
4. Reading/navigation and supported form filling.
5. Password workflows, local OCR, image workflows and safe document protection.
6. Corpus/performance/accessibility/recovery audits and packaging improvements.

Reorder based on observed failures and user steering. Each large subsystem gets a
separate design/plan; do not implement several unverified subsystems at once.

## Remaining review coverage notes

- Paragraph selection cap and deferred-composition cancellation after candidate creation
  are covered by cycle10a.
- Cycle10b adds automated actual-desktop rotated-workspace paragraph acceptance
  and independent pixel assertions that include the preserved comment icon.
These are recorded quality gaps, not evidence of observed data loss.

- Image importer: dimension guidance and active-Find regression completed in cycle10a;
  pending-picker unmount has smoke coverage and a reviewed mounted guard.
- Image conversion currently uses decoded RGB samples; print ICC color management
  and individual image-object editing remain separate milestones.
- Bookmarks: moving an existing branch to a different parent and executing external
  actions inside Folio are not implemented. Current nesting uses child creation;
  sibling movement preserves complete branches. Local checkpoint packaging is tracked
  separately from published releases; see the current cycle record above.
- Forms: direct page interaction, form authoring, calculations, complex form fonts
  and large-form resource/performance coverage remain open. Readonly, rich-text and
  file-selection fields are not editable. Cross-page shared fields block isolated
  source-text editing until document-wide replacement can preserve their identity.
  Signed/XFA rewriting and export-based printing are unsupported. Combining forms
  with name/action or document-script conflicts can require manual renaming.
  Complete JavaScript semantics, tagged/optional-content dependencies, legacy
  resource lookup and large-file/fuzz coverage are not established by this cycle.
  Deferred review minor: separate field events and widget events in merged AA
  dictionaries rather than carrying the full dictionary on both split objects.
- Protection: PDF contexts, native/UI permissions, password retry/owner unlock, restricted
  search and encrypted exports are integrated. Desktop lifecycle acceptance passes;
  review cleanup finding is fixed with native/UI regressions. Abandoned challenges
  expire on the next password operation; idle ciphertext housekeeping remains deferred.
  Signed/public-key workflows,
  general per-stream crypt overrides and filtered clear-metadata chains are unsupported.
  Limits have thin boundary/fuzz coverage and do not prove a whole-process memory cap.
  pypdf6.14.2 decrypts an Identity metadata body incorrectly; its dictionary/page checks
  pass, while MuPDF independently verifies both clear body and encrypted dictionary.
  Portable0.15.0-dev.1 includes protected workflows; the earlier0.14.0-dev.1 package is
  preserved separately.


## Verified image placement checkpoint 0.17 (2026-10-23)

PNG/JPEG insertion now has a native page preview, pointer/keyboard placement,
local move and resize drafts, shared proportion lock, numeric position/size and
quarter-turn rotation. Canceled work stays outside history. Original image tokens
transfer into workspace ownership and survive undo/redo without another acquisition.
A bounded128MiB prepared-image cache avoids repeated decoding/compression; native
permissions remain checked before cache access. Preview/print omit the original
editable-image attachment, while Save retains it.

Verification:403 native tests pass,4 ignored;588 frontend tests pass. One fresh
integrated review found two application integration defects (Enter intercepted
Cancel/Retry; Find left invisible placement active). Both are fixed with four
regressions connecting the real App and viewport. Existing86 independent PDF
cases pass. Actual Windows acceptance on a copy of the user's quiz passes native
PNG/JPEG import, EXIF, transparent appearance, move/resize/rotation, undo/redo,
editable/flattened save/reopen, restricted-reader controls, and forced-exit recovery
with unavailable original PDF/image copies. Original page drawing bytes and image
bytes are unchanged. MuPDF text is exactly unchanged; transparency adds at most
2/255 rounding outside image frames, and removing only the added image annotations
restores exact native pixels.

Release synthetic measurements: warm1200px8MP alpha composition improved from
1032ms to77ms; JPEG259ms to118ms. Cold first8MP alpha preview remains about593ms.
The coalesced queue avoids obsolete-frame buildup but these measurements do not
establish60fps dragging or a whole-process memory ceiling. Debug desktop mouse
gesture plus preview checks measured540ms/381ms including test-driven movement.

Portable0.17.0-dev.1 is built and verified:1148 archive files,566 dependency records,
packaged startup and the full image workflow including recovery. Independent
checks pass on the packaged outputs; package contents are unchanged after running.
Packaged pointer gesture plus preview measurements were207ms/191ms for the small
image and388ms/390ms for the8MP photo, including scripted mouse movement.
Portable0.16 and the user's original quiz remain preserved. Existing-image
replacement, crop, stacking controls, broader file/memory corpus, advanced forms
and signing, true redaction, and other parity gaps remain. The toolbar currently
scrolls horizontally at narrower window widths; reducing that friction is a
separate interface improvement.


Next cycle: improve toolbar layout so Save, undo/redo, zoom, Find and Print remain
visible at the supported window sizes. Keep image/text editing and document
creation actions accessible without requiring a horizontal toolbar scroll. This
is a bounded usability pass before the next larger PDF capability. Evidence is
the actual desktop screenshots in artifacts/image-controls-portable; the present
toolbar scrolls as an entire row, sometimes hiding primary actions. No native PDF
changes are implied by this next cycle.

## Automatic paragraph suggestions source checkpoint (2026-10-23)

validates font identity, size, color, ordering, page-copy appearance and permissions.
Suggestion creates no edited source/history. The dialog initializes text and area,
offers Adjust selection, and falls back to manual pieces for uncertain formatting.
Canceled or obsolete suggestions cannot replace current dialog state.

now groups those rows before the existing mixed-format refusal. Both failures and
earlier copied binaries remain recorded, not overwritten.

## Faithful paragraph re-edit checkpoint (2026-10-23 local)

Complete for supported Folio-created uniform paragraph edits. Save/reopen now
retains entered hard breaks, blank lines including their spaces, edge spaces,
the chosen area, alignment and line spacing. Automatic suggestion can recover the
complete paragraph from a visible row below a blank line. Partial selections and
foreign PDFs without valid hints continue using geometric inference.

Bounded page-local records store hashes, break counts and geometry, never a second
copy of paragraph words. Restoration validates actual operators, font/style,
effective text state, glyph coordinates and reproduced layout. Rotation, page
duplication/reorder and protected export retain settings. Limits remain 64 rows
per remembered paragraph, 64 records/page and 512 KiB; older hints can be evicted.

Three P2 review findings were reproduced and fixed: refreshed untrusted glyph
coordinates no longer authorize different character spacing; overlapping records
cannot choose a paragraph by JSON order; space-only hard lines roundtrip in original
and substitute fonts without relaxing foreign-PDF admission. Review and resolution:

## Mixed-paragraph model milestone (2026-10-23 local; feature incomplete)

Returned to the core existing-PDF editing gap. Three authored PDFs isolate mixed
font, size and color changes; native inspection rejects each combined paragraph
with the uniform-format message while accepting all nine pieces individually.
The unchanged private quiz has six MuPDF text styles, two mixed geometric blocks
and one vector drawing. These are inventory facts, not paragraph eligibility or
proof of safe highlighted-text reflow. No private text was copied into fixtures.

The full implementation plan is `superpowers/plans/2026-10-23-mixed-paragraphs.md`.
Native admission, resource selection, layout, output verification and re-edit hints
all assume one style today. The plan retains the full mixed-format objective and
separates an explicit attributed editing model, verified native spans/resources,
mixed layout/publication, independently checked metadata, and the styled editor UI.
Graphics/highlighting compatibility remains a separate fidelity requirement.

The internal attributed model is implemented in `src/editor/styled-paragraph.ts`.
It carries ordered text/style-ID runs, preserves unaffected formatting through
explicit UTF-16 range edits, uses deterministic typing-style inheritance, supports
range formatting/mixed-state queries, and owns frozen snapshots for undo. It
coalesces equal adjacent styles, preserves hard breaks and Unicode, validates
grapheme boundaries, and bounds the source palette and logical draft. Native
font/encoding admission is explicitly not established by these IDs.

Review caught valid CRLF pastes rejected before normalization; a failing regression
and corrected raw/normalized limits resolve it. An empty typing-style restoration
defect was also reproduced and corrected. Unicode seam tests require atomic
rejection of incompatible style joins, and a caret helper snaps positions after
successful accent/ZWJ/regional-indicator joins. Deserialized snapshots must pass
through the constructor. These contracts are prerequisites for UI integration.

## Native mixed-paragraph inspection milestone (2026-10-23 local; feature incomplete)

The engine now has a separate read-only `inspect_styled_paragraph` Rust API. It
returns logical attributed runs and a palette bound to verified source font
resources, effective sizes, and colors. Adjacent equal styles coalesce; inserted
separators have explicit provenance. Font aliases can share a style only when they
refer to the same verified resource; equal display names alone do not merge fonts.
Standard and embedded fonts, intrinsic rotations, cropping, and equivalent scaling
have focused evidence. Existing uniform callers retain their original path.

Review found that a later large piece on a line could make a valid paragraph fail
the spacing check. A failing regression confirmed it; admission now uses both
complete contiguous rows' maximum sizes. Another regression corrected inferred
word spaces being suppressed by a larger first row. Tight/excessive spacing remains
rejected. Boundary checks accept 64 pieces/4096 bytes and refuse 65/4097 without
truncation or source mutation.

## Native mixed-paragraph layout stage (2026-10-23 local; publication incomplete)

Added a read-only, source-bound layout staging API. It re-inspects the immutable
selection and validates expected text/style IDs before measuring source font
advances, ascent/descent and glyph ink. Words remain whole across formatting
changes. The deterministic kernel retains spaces and hard breaks, accounts for
negative bearings and tall/descending glyphs, and supports left/center/right and
soft-wrap-only justification. An interword space group gets one expansion even
when it crosses multiple styles. Blank rows retain break-style metrics. Minimum
leading uses both adjacent rows so automatic line boxes do not overlap.

A regression exposed the first hard row's larger font leaking into later soft
rows' heights. The corrected kernel uses each actual row's styles. Review found
no remaining concrete correctness defects. Added coverage exercises adversarial
later negative bearings plus earlier right overhangs, leading/final blank rows,
tall trailing spaces, UTF-8 byte boundaries, 64/65 styles and 4096 fragments.

## 2026-10-24 — Fit area to current paragraph text

The paragraph dialog now offers **Fit area to text**. It measures the current
draft with its original or explicitly substituted fonts, sizes and verified
highlights, then tries available width/height while keeping the raw PDF top/left
anchor. It avoids unselected text and graphics. Changed bounds are one undoable
step and require a new preview; already-fitting or failed requests preserve a
valid preview. The search is bounded and may refuse constrained areas.

### Native page content replacement — 2026-10-24

Added an immutable native API for replacing one page's content while retaining
destination page geometry/identity, annotations, form hierarchy, links and bookmarks.
Incoming content/resources are imported directly and fitted to the retained viewport;
same-aspect imports avoid introducing an unnecessary clipping restriction. Standard
and embedded text edits pass after supported replacements. Incoming annotations and
bookmarks are not imported; protected incoming PDFs, editable-overlay/OCR inputs,
tagged/layered/output-intent metadata and unsafe resource contexts reject explicitly.

Native API only: no IPC/UI command or new packaged executable yet. Preview/apply,
one-step undo, duplicate occurrence/source ownership, cross-version native links and

replacement as an available end-user feature. Follow-on architecture notes are in

object counts; embedded font bytes and edits, field filling and protected
save/reopen are checked. These are scoped tests, not a full-project/parity gate.

the original report and completed correction evidence remain available.

as sufficient. This deliberate rule change and raw evidence are documented.

## October 25 checkpoint: faster single-source PDF composition

Verified checkpoint: `artifacts/large-export-20261025/checkpoint/verification.json`.
Executable `8c3bc11eed09616e0b8fde0250d389bb9bd42dda96d7af9fe1eff45af70f1511`,
source fingerprint `c3c98177dc65b2fb92553c515201ee46ebc9d6104cec2746d25e68719427c09e`.

Phase profiling showed that five repeated strict parses dominated extraction from
our 10,000-page source; individual page importing was inexpensive. SourceForm now
privately retains one immutable parse for the duration of eligible single-source
composition, sharing it with form, link, resource and native-comment preservation.
Eligibility is <=8 MiB source bytes and <=100,000 parsed objects. Every validation,
authorization and traversal budget remains; combined/larger sources stay uncached.
The retained parse is dropped before final serialization and protection.

Three samples each at 1,000 and 10,000 pages were measured without simultaneous
builds/tests. At 10,000 pages, median five-page extraction improved 7.05 -> 1.56 s,
and full native export 11.75 -> 5.32 s. Sampled process peak private bytes increased
169,619,456 -> 210,444,288 (about 41 MB). This is a deliberate bounded-admission
latency/memory tradeoff, not a total heap-size guarantee. Highly compressed object
streams can allocate beyond the admission numbers; that coverage remains open.

346 native checks passed, five diagnostics ignored. Coverage includes forms,
bookmarks, protected exports/recovery, resource-name repair, crops, comments,
duplicate occurrences and existing text editing. Frozen-scope review found no
demonstrated defects. A fresh TypeScript/desktop build passed; frontend implementation
was unchanged, so its full suite was not redundantly rerun.

Packaged Windows acceptance rotated/extracted five far pages, moved them to the
start and saved the 10,000-page arrangement. Independent MuPDF verified all saved
page text/dimensions and representative exact pixels, including repeated native
benchmark outputs. An additional packaged save took 7.58 s including dialog and
automation (6.22 s after the dialog helper returned). The attempted IPC wrapper
collected no events, so there is no claimed stage attribution for that desktop run.
The preceding >60 s observation remains unreproduced/unexplained. The second PDF
has fresh IDs/timestamps; after a byte-equality shortcut failed, full semantic and
sampled raster comparison passed. Originals, failed logs and prior builds preserved.
Loaded PDFium matched; owned PID 73464 stopped/absence verified; screenshot inspected.

Next: measure compressed-object-stream memory and repeated long-lived saves before
expanding retention. Broader PDF producer/annotation/security matrices and the
unexplained earlier desktop delay remain gaps. This is verified incremental progress,
not release approval or Adobe/Foxit parity.


## October 25 follow-up: compressed-source and repeated-save memory

Verification: `artifacts/export-memory-20261025/verification.json`. Production source

existing document authorization and preservation checks.

**Fill forms → Text appearance…** now shows the original font, physical font size,
Auto fit, alignment, and Original color versus explicit Custom RGB. The selected
widget gets a native preview and one-step Apply/Undo/Redo. Shared values, sibling
appearances, source fonts, field metadata/actions, geometry, protection and page
occurrences are preserved within the admitted cases. Unchanged size/color settings
are omitted from requests. Original CMYK/gray is not implicitly converted to RGB.

The final review identified three defects. Public regressions reproduced custom
paint loss, visible text settings differing from DA, and cleanup through a changed
adapter. Corrections conservatively admit ordinary text and dictionary-backed solid
rectangular borders/backgrounds; check visible fixed size/color and neutral text
scaling; account for Auto fit; and retain the producing adapter with each candidate.
Custom painting, altered paint order/clipping and ambiguous visible/default settings
are refused. The original review and parent response are retained; no rereview.

Corrected evidence: **45 native tests** across appearance/filling/positioning/reflow,
**1,215 frontend tests** in184 files, TypeScript/Vite/native build and unchanged build
fingerprint. Native coverage includes empty, embedded, single-line, multiline and
plain comb fields; AP-bound font-program identity; shared/repeated occurrences;
UserUnit1/1.7/2 and rotations; unchanged fractional CMYK; Auto fit save→reopen→fill;
readonly fields, alternate artwork, protected copies and repeated-edit retention.
Twelve saved PDFs were independently checked for settings and unchanged sibling/page
pixels. The prior972-test full native renderer run is baseline evidence, not a new
full native run for this feature.

The corrected Windows executable passed size/alignment/color preview, Apply,
Undo/Redo, later filling, save/reopen, Auto fit and a640px empty-field workflow.
Two adversarial PDFs displayed a refusal with disabled Preview/Apply and unchanged
history/source. Independent PyMuPDF/pypdf checks verified all four desktop outputs,
including actual CMYK operators (PyMuPDF's widget-color accessor does not report
CMYK correctly), complete shared values, metadata/actions, rectangles and unchanged
page paint. Owned process53672 was stopped. Earlier harness locator failures remain
recorded; they were not product defects.

Limits: original font only; conservative AP support; some valid producer-specific
artwork, borders, transforms or visible/default disagreements cannot be restyled.
Fixed-size text can clip. No exhaustive malformed/alias/flag/crop matrix, broad
producer/performance/printing/accessibility acceptance, or Adobe/Foxit parity claim.
The next cycle should prioritize broader real-PDF editing compatibility and field
definition controls, using the remaining review coverage gaps to choose concrete cases.

## Choice editor native model — implementation in progress

The native value-model foundation now resolves edits by original row identity,
preserving current selection through label/export changes and reordering. Removed
selected/default rows require explicit replacement or clearing. Duplicate export
defaults retain value semantics without inventing a unique row; free-text answers
cannot silently become option selections. Sorted fields require explicit manual
order for row changes. Option/value counts and combined output payloads are bounded
before text cloning; unknown transport fields and invalid identities are refused.

Model gate: **259 library and 17 model tests passed, 3 ignored**, with source
fingerprint `8a075d58be66fd61963471370a0cec8ace83a737a918173c2833174d37558ced`.
Only the new native model and lib exports changed in the product fingerprint.
Evidence is in `artifacts/form-choice-model-20261025/verification.json`.
The missing-API red test and intermediate lifetime compile error remain preserved.

This is not an available desktop feature. Source-bound PDF inspection/mutation,
raw-string budgets, native selected-value confirmation, font/artwork preservation,
preview/Apply/UI, history, save/reopen/recovery and final review remain required.
The latest verified executable is still the recovery checkpoint. No new app build
or desktop acceptance is claimed for the model alone.

### Source-bound choice inspection

The next native layer now reads paired export/display options, current/default
values and linked widget locations from the validated logical field hierarchy.
Source/page/widget keys bind inspection to exact input bytes. Empty/free-text,
duplicate-export and inherited values are distinguished; malformed encodings,
ambiguous selection and inconsistent saved values/indices are refused.

Parent-link mistakes. Dropdowns also retain harmless list-only metadata while
inspection follows PDFium's effective single-select/top-index behavior. Repeated
placements of one widget object are listed separately. Existing field-property
editing keeps its original inheritance behavior and passes its regression suite.

and11existing-properties. Eight PDF fixture variants compare labels/selection with

`ab238b0e99c09534218d4cdcb33f6402146ef497e6656a3adf3dfab989dd940b`.

or completed choice-editor capability is claimed.

## 2026-10-26: Existing form field removal

Whole logical fields can now be removed from Fill forms, with the affected linked
appearances and source pages shown before applying. All copies of a source in the
active document change together; other tabs and original files retain their
sources. Undo/Redo, editable export and reopen are verified. Printed page text
and artwork remain. Individual widgets/radio options are a future scope.

The native edit prunes empty ancestors and calculation-order entries, copies shared
arrays privately, and refuses surviving action/structure references. Selected alias
identities and script-dependent forms are refused. Three independent review P2
findings were reproduced and fixed, with exact evidence in
[the verification record](../artifacts/form-removal-20261026/verification.json).
The corrected native scope passed 22 tests. The full frontend suite passed 1,269 tests
with four workers; two later lifecycle checks also passed. An initial five-second
timeout in the existing 2,000-option dialog test is retained; no timeout was raised.
The final Windows workflow covers Cancel, linked removal across copied pages,
Undo/Redo, save and reopen. MuPDF/pypdf checks found no differences outside removed
widget rectangles across 12 page-scale comparisons at 1x/2x/4x.

### 2026-10-26: existing field borders and backgrounds verified

The frame milestone now includes the native immutable transaction and UI preview
controls for text, checkbox, radio, dropdown and list fields. Solid/dashed radio
circles and solid/dashed/underline/beveled/inset rectangle frames preserve proved
source paint, aliases and selected page occurrences. Original palettes and dash
paths remain intact; zero/transparent borders can be restored. Unsupported source
artwork, ambiguous states and borders overlapping a radio answer remain refusals.

The one final review found two preservation defects. Caption text outside the
answer region now blocks whole-appearance reflow; Scale saved appearance retains
it. Background-only edits preserve the original border program; changed dashed
circles retain their path start, traversal and closure. Both findings have failing
reproductions followed by green tests. No second reviewer was used.
